@@120441110 <h> OPINION <h> SA faces a costly slap-down by Trump <p> William Saunderson-Meyer says the bill for years of Israel-baiting is about to fall due <p> JAUNDICED EYE <p> The jeering response of much of the legacy media 's commentariat to Donald Trump 's inauguration as the 47th president of the United States has been something to behold . <p> Aside from a few predictably conservative opinion writers , the tone , including in South Africa , has been relentlessly hostile and sneering , seething with anger and barely controlled agitation . Such emotional distress is a measure of the degree to which journalists , across the political spectrum , have abandoned their traditional role of trying to be dispassionate observers in favour of being influencers of events , whatever the cost to the now all but abandoned ideals of balance and truth . <p> Of course , their dismay is not entirely unjustified . For the next four years , the still most politically consequential country in the world will be led -- barring a divinely hurled lightning bolt or a more capable assassin than the two who 've @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ exaggerated opinion of his own and his country 's power . <p> In South Africa , much of the concern revolves around what cost we might have to pay for an African National Congress ( ANC ) foreign policy which , dressed up as ' non-alignment ' and ' multi-polarity ' , has consistently acted to damage the interests of the Western bloc in support of Russia , China and Iran , as well the likes of Cuba , Venezuela and Zimbabwe . The Government of National Unity ( GNU ) is putting a great deal of store in the same things that the National Party government thought would make it immune to Western pressure : SA 's strategically important geographic position and its bountiful supplies of economically vital minerals . <p> ANC thinking is based on the belief that Trump , the ultimate deal-maker , will be entirely transactionalist in foreign policy . Their assumption is that as long as SA continues to ship to the US the rare earth minerals it needs for its electronics and military sector , all that the GNU needs to do is tone @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ carefully . The African Growth and Opportunity Act , under which SA annually exports to the US vehicles , motor parts , agricultural products and textiles valued at close to $3 billion may very well survive . However , it 's less likely that the money the US has pledged to SAs Just Energy Transition Partnership -- $1 billion of the $8.5 billion package -- will be forthcoming . <p> --&gt; <p> This week , at the World Economic Forum in Davos , President Cyril Ramaphosa brushed off these concerns . He told reporters that SA enjoyed good relations with the US , its second-largest trading partner after China , and he and Trump would work closely to address climate financing and other issues . ' I 'm sure that we will find great solutions , ' Ramaphosa beamed . ' President Trump is a great dealmaker and so am I. ' <p> Ramaphosa should n't be too sanguine . @@120547792 <h> OPINION <h> The genocide libel label <p> Dominique Herman says accusation against Israel 's conduct of the war against Hamas persists in the unlikeliest of places <p> The claim that Israel committed genocide in Gaza persists even in the unlikeliest of forums . <p> I spent last weekend in Trier , Germany 's oldest city , a place rich in Roman ruins and UNESCO World Heritage Sites as well as the oldest church in the country . <p> Jewish life in the city dates back to the Roman era but it was in the early 14th century that the city 's small Jewish community really thrived . In 1349 , though , pogroms put paid to Jewish life there and about 70 years later , any Jews remaining were banished entirely from the archdiocese . <p> Only three centuries later were Jews allowed to settle again in Trier , in their still tiny numbers . They lived there then as Jews live today wherever there are Jewish communities in countries around the world : as small but well integrated minorities . <p> Then Hitler came to power in Germany @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ murdered . In the 1990s , the influx of Jews from the former Soviet Union formed the basis for yet another revival of Jewish community life in Trier , and now there is a new synagogue there to replace the one that was looted and burned by the National Socialists in 1938 during Pogromnacht , or Kristallnacht as it 's more commonly known . <p> The history of Jews in Trier is Jewish history in a nutshell : a pattern of devastation and drumming out followed by returning and rebuilding . Rinse and repeat . And so when I read that " genocide continues " in an allusion to Gaza , I felt supremely weary . Especially since I read it in the first sentence of a cookery newsletter to which I subscribe that , up till then , had dealt exclusively with topics such as Bundt cakes and baba ganoush . <p> --&gt; <p> Genocide is about intention . If Israel wanted to commit genocide in Gaza , it could . It certainly has the military might to do so . In this conflict , though , it 's @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ) who have consistently and loudly declared their genocidal intent , and it 's a wonder that this still needs to be pointed out . <p> If Hamas had its way , the destruction of Israel and the driving into the sea of every Jew in Israel would be a highly desirable outcome . A desire made abundantly clear by the barbarism it unleashed on civilian Israelis in October 2023 . A desire also expressed repeatedly by its leaders when interviewed on television , and by what is stated in its founding charter . As Maya Angelou said , when someone shows you who they are , believe them the first time . <p> Retired British Army officer Richard Kemp wrote in the UK Telegraph the day after the ceasefire saw the first three of the remaining Israeli hostages released from Hamas captivity : <p> " Since the cessation was agreed the terrorist leaders have been renewing their vows to kill more Jews and launch repeated 7th October style massacres . Meanwhile hordes of Gazan civilians have flooded into the streets , mostly healthy in appearance , well dressed and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Hamas , the UN , human rights groups and so much of the media have painted for the last 15 months : of disease and famine at the hands of the Israelis . <p> --&gt; <p> Despite claims of fuel and energy starvation in Gaza , cars now seem to be able to freely drive the people about and their ubiquitous smart phones are obviously well charged . Like their Hamas leaders , these ' innocent ' civilians have been screaming themselves hoarse with ' Khaybar , Khaybar ya yahud ' , an Arabic rallying cry calling for the slaughter of Jews . " <p> When it comes to being on the receiving end of genocide , Jews know a thing or two about it . When Hitler came to power in 1933 , 9.5 million Jews lived in Europe . That figure constituted more than 60 percent of the world 's entire Jewish population . By 1945 , Hitler and his henchmen had managed to kill two out of every three Jews on the continent . In the German Reich , where he was the F ? hrer and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 000 to a couple of tens of thousands . <p> The Nazis did it with intent and they did it systematically . They did it by rounding people up and carting them off in cattle carriers to concentration camps where they were herded into gas chambers and murdered en masse . It was a masterclass in logistics . <p> The Henry Jackson Society think-tank released its in-depth findings into the fatality figures disseminated by Hamas during the war and discovered all manner of discrepancies and creative accounting ( page 5 provides a neat summation ) . Bearing in mind that the source for these figures is a group of men that delights in killing Jewish children in front of their parents , and slicing off Jewish women 's breasts and playing catch with them , it should hardly come as a surprise that they 're not entirely trustworthy . <p> --&gt; <p> But even if one was to take at face value Hamas-reported deaths during the course of this year-and-a-quarter period , 46 000 Palestinians have been killed in the Gaza Strip during the war , according to the latest @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ around 2.2 million , making Israel evidently quite lousy at genocide considering its highly sophisticated capabilities as an army . <p> And that figure of 46 000 does not distinguish between civilians and combatants either -- the latter number estimated at more than 17 000 -- or deaths that would have occurred anyway due to old age or long-standing illness , estimated at 5 000 . Not to mention fatalities from misfired rocket launches by Hamas and other combatant groups in Gaza that were attributed to Israel . <p> Sheer incompetence could be the reason that the number of casualties in Gaza does not stack up against the slur of genocide against Israel . But a defence force that managed to pull off a manoeuvre fit for a Mission Impossible movie -- the simultaneous detonation of thousands of handheld pagers and hundreds of walkie-talkies belonging to Hezbollah terrorists in Lebanon and Syria -- makes that an unlikely conclusion . <p> In the course of the war , Israel has had to pursue terrorist savages who hide in a densely-packed urban environment . While Hamas employs every measure conceivable to put @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ goes to multifarious lengths to warn Gazan civilians of danger . No other army in the world does that and if the IDF was endeavouring to commit genocide , going out of its way to alert civilians to impending airstrikes is a strange way indeed to go about it . <p> This column first appeared on Dominique Herman 's Substack , 24 January 2025

@@120583309 <h> Houthis release Galaxy Leader crew after over a year in captivity <p> Yemen 's Houthis have released the 25 crew of the Bahamas flagged car carrier Galaxy Leader , 14 months after it was seized off Yemen 's Red Sea coast . <p> The crew are from Bulgaria , Ukraine , the Philippines , Mexico , and Romania . They were held captive from 19 November 2023 when Houthi forces boarded the vessel at sea via helicopter and escorted it into the port of Hodeidah . They targeted the vessel due to its apparent links to Israeli billionaire Abraham ' Rami ' Ungar ( the vessel is owned by Galaxy Maritime Ltd , which is owned by Ray Car Carriers , which is co-owned by Ungar ) . <p> International Maritime Organisation ( IMO ) Secretary-General Arsenio Dominguez welcomed the release of the vessel 's crew . " This is a moment of profound relief for all of us -- not only for the crew and their families , but also to the wider maritime community . <p> " Today 's breakthrough is a testament to the power of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ not become collateral victims in wider geopolitical tensions . It is also a return to operations in the Red Sea as we have been accustomed to , and upholding of the freedom of navigation . " <p> The US Department of State expressed deep gratitude to the Sultanate of Oman for its " essential and timely efforts to secure the release of the crew " on Wednesday . <p> " The Houthis had unlawfully detained the crew since violently seizing their vessel over a year ago . @@120621653 <h> Doomsday Clock Moves Closer To Midnight Than Ever Before <p> The unique timepiece was created seventy-eight years ago as a symbolic attempt to gauge how close humanity is to destroying the world , with midnight representing the moment when people will have made the Earth uninhabitable . <p> The Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists has announced that the Doomsday Clock has been moved forward to 89 seconds to midnight . This is the closest to " Doomsday " it 's ever been . <p> The unique timepiece was created seventy-eight years ago as a symbolic attempt to gauge how close humanity is to destroying the world , with midnight representing the moment when people will have made the Earth uninhabitable . <p> For the last two years the clock has been set at 90 seconds to midnight , mainly because of the Ukraine/Russia war , the potential of a nuclear arms race , the Israel-Hamas conflict in Gaza , and the climate crisis . Although by no means a definitive countdown to our demise , the clock has rather served to spark conversations about difficult scientific topics such as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ It 's an imperfect metaphor . " <p> Daniel Holz , the Bulletin 's science and security board chair and professor in the Department of Physics , Astronomy and Astrophysics at the University of Chicago , said " We set the clock closer to midnight because we do not see sufficient , positive progress on the global challenges we face , including nuclear risk , climate change , biological threats and advances in disruptive technologies . " <p> " Trends that have deeply concerned the Science and Security Board continued , and despite unmistakable signs of danger , national leaders and their societies have failed to do what is needed to change course . Consequently , we now move the Doomsday Clock from 90 seconds to 89 seconds to midnight -- the closest it has ever been to catastrophe . " <p> During a news briefing Tuesday , Holz said that the countries that possess nuclear weapons are increasing the size of their arsenals and investing hundreds of billions of dollars in weapons that can destroy civilization many times over . " <p> Besides the familiar threat of nukes , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ -- the spread of misinformation , disinformation and conspiracy theories , as a further reason why the clock has been set forward . These three issues " degrade the communication ecosystem and increasingly blur the line between truth and falsehood , " Holz added . <p> Moving the clock back is however possible . The hand moved the farthest away from midnight -- a whopping 17 minutes before the hour -- when then-President George H.W. Bush 's administration signed the Strategic Arms Reduction Treaty with the Soviet Union in 1991 . <p> Image : Britannica <p> The Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists was founded by a group of scientists who worked on the Manhattan Project , the code name for the development of the atomic bomb during World War II . Originally , the organization was conceived to measure nuclear threats , but in 2007 it made the decision to include climate change in its calculations . <p> " While I wish we could go back to talking about minutes to midnight instead of seconds , unfortunately that no longer reflects reality . @@120708416 <p> President Cyril Ramaphosa , left , and Rwanda President Paul Kagame are shown at a meeting in April 2024 in this file photo . Picture : LUKE DRAY/GALLO IMAGES <p> In a striking social media post on January 29 Rwandan President Paul Kagame took aim at President Cyril Ramaphosa , accusing him of misrepresenting their recent conversation about the situation in Goma , capital of the Democratic Republic of Congo 's ( DRC ) North Kivu province . <p> At the heart of the dispute is the conflict in the region , where SA troops , part of a regional peacekeeping force , are engaged with Rwanda-backed rebels . These rebels claim to be protecting the Tutsi population from Hutu extremists , the group that was responsible for the 1994 Rwandan genocide . <p> Kagame 's anger was sparked by several aspects of Ramaphosa 's statement , which had been shared on social media . The SA president claimed that 13 members of the SA National Defence Force ( SANDF ) had been killed by the Rwanda-backed rebels , not by friendly fire as he and Kagame had purportedly @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ with Ramaphosa 's characterisation of the Rwandan military as a " militia " and the regional force in the DRC as a peacekeeping mission . In a combative tone , Kagame declared that SA was in no position to mediate the conflict , and that Rwanda would not hesitate to respond to any confrontations from the SA government . <p> This flare-up between the two leaders is emblematic of the deeper tension between Rwanda and SA that has been simmering for years , but has recently been stoked by the situation in the DRC . <p> For more than a decade SA has offered sanctuary to dissidents from authoritarian regimes , including those critical of Kagame 's government who it has sought to silence . The most notable example was the 2014 assassination of Kagame 's former ally-turned-critic Patrick Karegeya in Johannesburg , an act widely believed to have been orchestrated by Rwandan agents . <p> The murder , which followed a failed attempt on another Kagame critic , Kayumba Nyamwasa , was a turning point . It led to the expulsion of Rwandan diplomats from SA , and Rwanda @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ had gradually normalised by 2019 with the reopening of SA 's embassy in Rwanda , the two countries have remained on opposite sides of key international issues . <p> SA 's unwavering support of the International Criminal Court ( ICC ) , and its recent push for an investigation into alleged Israeli war crimes in Gaza , is another point of contention . Kagame , by contrast , has been vocal in his criticism of the ICC and has expressed support for Israel 's right to self-defence . Differences in views on human rights , governance and the reform of the AU only add fuel to the fire . <p> However , the rhetoric from Kagame is not just about ideological differences . It speaks volumes about his broader ambitions in the DRC . <p> Despite Kagame 's combative rhetoric , it seems unlikely that Rwanda and SA will engage in direct hostilities . <p> While he has expressed a willingness to engage in peacebuilding discussions , his long-term goal in the DRC appears to be the establishment of a proxy government in the east . This would serve as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ which have taken refuge in North and South Kivu , and would give Rwanda access to valuable mineral resources in the region . <p> This geopolitical game is not just about military dominance but about securing Rwanda 's strategic interests . These interests would be better served with SA 's departure from the DRC , where its presence serves as a key stumbling block to Kagame 's aspirations . <p> But what does this spat mean for Rwanda-SA relations ? Despite Kagame 's combative rhetoric , it seems unlikely that Rwanda and SA will engage in direct hostilities . Both nations understand the catastrophic consequences of such an escalation.For Kagame , the goal may not be full-scale war with SA but rather the gradual removal of SANDF troops from the region . <p> SA , on the other hand , faces increasing domestic pressure to withdraw its forces , particularly as the SANDF grapples with resource shortages . Any major hostilities would risk drawing in other countries and destabilising the region even further , something both Rwanda and SA are keen to avoid . <p> The more probable outcome is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for a d ? marche , perhaps recalling its diplomats or even expelling the Rwandan envoy , actions that would undoubtedly be reciprocated by Kigali . Such a diplomatic fallout would reverberate beyond politics and into the economic realm . SA companies operating in Rwanda , like MTN and Shoprite , could face retaliation in the form of anticompetitive behaviour by the Rwandan state . <p> That said , developments such as a severance in trade ties or reciprocal entry restrictions on Rwandan and SA nationals would probably occur only in a dramatic escalation from the current level of acrimony -- a scenario that is not immediately anticipated . <p> While the battle for influence in the DRC unfolds , one thing is clear : Kagame and Ramaphosa are locked in a high-stakes game where every move , every statement , carries significant weight -- not just for the Great Lakes region , but for their nations ' broader geopolitical futures . 
@@120708423 <p> The SA National Defence Force presence in the DRC has been marred by the deaths of 13 soldiers . Picture : SUNDAY TIMES/ALISTAIR RUSSELL <p> SA often uses its diplomatic heft to position itself as defender of the Global South on the world stage , but the deaths of 13 of its soldiers in eastern Democratic Republic of Congo ( DRC ) fighting have exposed an inability to project hard power in its own backyard . <p> The SA government has challenged US hegemony , taken a strong stand against Israel 's actions in Gaza and is intent on defending the interests of developing nations , notably as it assumes the Group of 20 ( G20 ) presidency this year . <p> However , in Africa , its efforts to exert influence have been plagued by missteps over the past decade . <p> In the DRC this week , Rwandan-backed M23 rebels seized Goma , the country 's largest eastern city . A force composed of SA troops and Southern African allies tasked with stopping the insurgents has taken heavy losses and is surrounded without a clear exit strategy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ standing on the continent , " said Liesl Louw-Vaudran , senior adviser on the AU for the International Crisis Group , a nonprofit group that researches and offers ways to resolve armed conflicts . <p> Far from uniting African nations , the week 's events have driven a wedge between President Cyril Ramaphosa and Rwandan President Paul Kagame , arguably the continent 's two most high-profile leaders . <p> The two men have sparred over social media . Ramaphosa has blamed the fighting on M23 and the Rwandan army .
